Page 4 of 7 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
9. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES (49 C.F.R. Part 26) 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin, or sex. 
 
10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, the Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a 
written statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, 
and work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing of 
any payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities that 
may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing in this 
Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or employees to 
act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any obligation of 
any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, debts, 
and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default. In the event the default remains 
uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from date of execution to December 31, 2027.  The 
City may extend the project for an additional three (3) years to December 31, 2030. 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 5-25 
Landfill Compliance Monitoring Services 
Page 5 of 7 
 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty (30) days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. Upon 
receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the Consultant 
for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of termination. 
Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, the 
City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of default 
of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default to 
commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear its 
own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming. The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
18. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary for 
the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the 
Agreement. Furthermore, the City specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et 
seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, 
enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of 
governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not 
be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to 
governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental 
immunity. 
 
19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are 
performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited 
to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring experts 
and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in progress; to 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 5-25 
Landfill Compliance Monitoring Services

examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this Agreement; 
and to observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work. 
 
20. 
OWNERSHIP OF DOCUMENTS AND WORK PRODUCT 
All deliverables by the Consultant, all work product of the Consultant, all notes, 
calculations, memoranda, or any other documentation generated by the Consultant 
pursuant to the scope of this Agreement, shall be the property of the City of Cheyenne and 
shall be provided to the City of Cheyenne immediately upon request. 
 
21. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With 
Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age 
Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto, 
and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color, 
race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection 
with the performance under this Agreement. 
 
22. 
FORCE MAJEURE. 
The performance of the Agreement by either party shall be subject to force majeure 
including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural disaster, war or threat of war, 
acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized strikes, governmental regulation or 
advisory, recognized health threats as determined by the World Health Organization, the 
Centers for Disease Control, or local government authority or health agencies (including, 
but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-19, H1N1, or similar infectious diseases), 
curtailment of transportation facilities, or other similar occurrence beyond the control of 
the parties, where any of those factors, circumstances, situations, or conditions or similar 
ones prevent, dissuade, or unreasonably delay the performance required by this Agreement. 
The Agreement may be cancelled by either party, without liability, damages, fees, or 
penalty, and any unused deposits or amounts paid shall be refunded, for any one or more 
of the above reasons, by written notice to the other party. 
 
23. 
PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION 
The Consultant shall endorse, as required by law, plans and reports prepared under this 
Agreement and shall affix thereto his or her seal of professional registration, showing that 
he or she is licensed to practice in the State of Wyoming, if necessary. 
 
24. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement consisting of seven (7) pages, Exhibit A – Compensation Schedule, 
consisting of fifteen (15) pages; Request for Proposal RFP 5-25, issued via OpenGov E-
Procurement Portal; and the Consultant’s submitted proposal, consisting of six hundred, 
eighty-four (684) pages, contains the entire understanding of the parties. There are no other 
terms or conditions, written or oral, concerning or controlling this matter.

Dear Mr. Barttelbort: 
Trihydro Corporation (Trihydro) appreciates the opportunity to submit a price proposal to continue 
providing professional compliance monitoring services to the City of Cheyenne (City) for the Happy Jack 
Landfill (Landfill) for 2025 through 2027, with a possible three-year extension.  We prepared the 
proposal based on information in the Request for Proposals-5-25 (RFP) released January 22, 2025, the 
Question-and-Answer document published February 10, 2025, and our site-specific knowledge from 
22 years of assisting the City with regulatory compliance at the Landfill.  The proposed work will be 
completed in accordance with the Sanitary Landfill Environmental Monitoring Plan (SLEMP), last 
revised March 14, 2019.  The Trihydro contact information is as follows: 
Trihydro Corporation 
1252 Commerce Drive 
Laramie, WY  82070 
Phone:  (307)-745-7474 
Primary Contact: Caroline Brewer, P.G. 
Attachment A in this price proposal includes Exhibit B from the RFP and presents a general summary of 
costs by task as designated by the City.  A more detailed breakdown of costs, activities, and assumptions 
is provided in Attachment B.  Attachment C includes our 2025 Schedule of Charges, and Attachment D is 
the RFP Attachment “B-1” – Use of Sub-Consultants, which identifies Eurofins as a subcontractor under 
this project.  As requested in RFP-5-25, Trihydro certifies that our price proposal, time, and materials not 
to exceed $281,007 for the three-year contract period will remain valid for a period of at least one 
hundred and twenty (120) days.  Note that, although years four through six are also included at a fixed 
price in Exhibit B – and assume an appropriate adjustment to account for inflation between each contract 
period – we ask that the City consider allowing Trihydro to re-evaluate costs in three years’ time in the 
case that inflation is more than anticipated.

Sincerely,

MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins, City of Cheyenne 
 
 
City Council Members 
 
FROM: 
Rick Amen, PE, City Traffic Engineer  
 
 
DATE:  
March 13, 2025 
 
SUBJECT: 
Cooperative Agreement between Wyoming Department of 
Transportation and the City of Cheyenne 
 
Federal Project No. B251A02 
 
Various Locations, Thermoplastic Striping, Laramie County  
 
 
Please find attached the proposed Cooperative Agreement for Federal Project No. B251A02, 
Various Locations, Thermoplastic Striping in Laramie County provided by WYDOT to the City 
of Cheyenne for your approval and execution.  The agreement is summarized below for your 
information.   
 
Purpose of Project: 
In conjunction with intersections planned to be restriped with thermoplastic markings by 
WYDOT, the city determined it would be in its best interest to upgrade the markings on the 
city roadways associated with the shared WYDOY/City intersections for improved 
consistency and a best approach to providing improved safety to the public.  Markings 
along Lincolnway, College Drive and at the intersection of Logan & Nationway were 
identified to be in need of upgraded thermoplastic markings as part of this project. 
 
Purpose of Agreement: 
The primary purpose of the agreement is to set forth the terms and conditions by the City 
will reimburse WYDOT for costs associated with thermoplastic restriping of crosswalks 
and other street markings at various locations throughout the City.  The locations are 
shown on Exhibit A, which is attached and in the agreement.  This project is associated with 
WYDOT’s main project B251010. 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
CITY ENGINEER’S OFFICE 
2101 O’Neil Avenue  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 

Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins 
Page 2 of 2 
March 18, 2025

Responsibilities: 
City of Cheyenne. 
 Reimburse WYDOT in accordance with Section 4 of the agreement. 
 Submit a letter to WYDOT designating a qualified Project representative, at no cost 
to WYDOT, capable of making timely decisions and authorized to sign documents 
concerning the construction of the Project. 
 WYDOT will give the City the opportunity to review and comment on Project cost 
estimates prior to the advertisement of bids. 
 WYDOT shall ask the City to concur in the award of the Project to the lowest 
qualified bidder.  As a result of signing the Letter of Concurrence, the City agrees to 
amend estimated costs to match the actual bid amount. 
 Upon completion and acceptance of the Project, the City shall maintain, at its sole 
expense, all features constructed under the Agreement.  Maintenance shall include 
all repairs necessary to keep the improvement in its functional constructed 
condition. 
 Identify areas of potential effect to and perform environmental studies (in 
compliance with 23 CFR 771) outside of the existing WYDOT Project limits.  Provide 
this information to WYDOT prior to the due date to be provided by WYDOT. 
 Approve final plans and estimates prior to advertisement by WYDOT. 
 
WYDOT. 
 Conduct preliminary engineering investigations and develop final design plans and 
estimates. 
 Perform construction engineering for the Project in accordance with Project plans 
and specifications. 
 Advertise the Project and award a bid to the lowest qualified bidder in accordance 
with WYDOT policies. 
 Review environmental information provided by the City and include or re-evaluate 
the NEPA document as necessary. 
 
Estimated Cost: 
The Total City Portion of the project including construction administration and 
construction cost estimated by WYDOT in February 2025 is $234,845.00 from the Optional 
One Percent Sales Tax (2023 – 2026) 5th Penny.

MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne 
 
FROM: 
Erin Gates 
 
DATE: 
March 5, 2025 
 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Service Agreement Contract Modification No. 1 between the City of 
Cheyenne and Benchmark Engineers for additional engineering services for 
Christensen Road Design Modification project.  
 
 
Please find attached Contract Modification No. 1 to the Professional Service Agreement between 
the City of Cheyenne and Benchmark Engineers for additional professional services related to the 
Christensen Road design modifications.  
  
Engineering staff evaluated the scope of services and corresponding fee proposal for the 
professional services for this specific project. The services to be provided are construction 
administration associated with approved plans to address storm water drainage improvements, 
updated signage and lighting at the intersection of Christensen Road and East Pershing Boulevard, 
and to plat the rights-of-way for the corridor and annex the roadway into the City of Cheyenne. 
 
Funding for this project will come from the 2017 Specific Purpose Optional Purpose Tax (6th 
Penny). The total professional services will be a cost not-to-exceed $52,836.00. We respectfully 
request your approval of this contract modification. 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
CITY ENGINEER’S OFFICE 
2101 O’Neil Avenue  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 

CITY OF CHEYENNE 
CONTRACT MODIFICATION 
 
Contract Modification: 
#1 
Project: 
 
 
Christensen Road Design Modification 
Contract Number: 
 
7520 
Project Number: 
 
180010 
Contractor: 
 
 
Benchmark Engineers, PC 
Date Prepared: 
 
March 3, 2025 
 
The above-referenced contract is amended or modified as set forth in the following and these 
amendments and modifications of the plans and specifications are agreed to by the parties. All 
other aspects of the original agreement remain unchanged and in full force and effect. 
 
I. Description of Changes 
 
Provide professional services associated with bidding and construction administration services 
for drainage, signage, and intersection lighting upgrades. Provide professional services 
associated with platting and annexation of the rights-of-way of Christensen Road between 
Commerce Circle and US Highway 30. Services include preparation of all necessary documents 
and serving as agent for these actions. Services are further described in Exhibit A, which is 
incorporated herein by reference, but do not include any provisions that were manually stricken 
in that exhibit by the City Engineer, dated February 27, 2025. 
 
II. New Contract Items 
 
Provide services associated with bidding and construction administration for the improvements 
show on the approved EPR-24-00019 plans to address stormwater drainage, upgraded signage 
and replacement of lighting at the intersection of Christensen Road and E. Pershing Boulevard. 
 
Platting un-platted rights-of-way of Christensen Road from Commerce Circle to US Highway 
30. Annex un-annexed portions of Christensen Road rights-of-way between Commerce Circle 
and US Highway 30 into the City of Cheyenne. 
 
III. Schedule 
 
Project schedule is extended until the work is completed. 
 
IV. Summary

AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-15-25 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, 2025, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the "CITY", and QUALITY 
ELECTRIC, hereinafter referred to as the "CONTRACTOR". 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the "Cheyenne Fire Rescue - Installation of Owner Provided 
Backup Generators for Stations #2 & #6" project all in strict accordance with the Contract 
Documents. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the "Notice to Proceed" that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by June 20, 2025.  If the work has not been completed 
within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City for excusable 
delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated damages, as 
stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the work is 
completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, in an amount of One 
Hundred Forty-Nine Thousand, Four Hundred Sixty-Seven Dollars ($149,467.00). In the 
event there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices 
multiplied by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted 
accordingly.  The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual services in the following 
manner, upon receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for the percentage of 
estimated work completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the 
payable due date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request 
shall be submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or 
the AIA Documents G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the estimate for approval 
prior to payment. 
11:44 am, Mar 04, 2025

Page 2 of 2 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the 
work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all 
Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of 
Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be 
made until the above documents have been received by the City and all items on the Punch 
List have been completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the

5. Supplemental Federal Provisions &

7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices;

Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has authorized the

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-02-04_TotalExtraction_96124710.pdf
1 
 
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 10, 2025  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting. Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Dr. Kathy Emmons, Ken 
Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mark Moody, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal and Jeff White. Absent: 
Tom Segrave. Also present: Kylie Soden, City Clerk. The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda. Dr. Aldrich moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Esquibel. Motion 
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 27, 2025. Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §15-9-208(c) on the 
Plan of Development for the Downtown Development Area. Public comments were made by: 
Astrid and Glen Garrett. Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: 
Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Dr. Emmons, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Moody, Mr. Roybal 
and Mr. White. There being no further comments or input and the purpose of the hearing having 
been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 15.08, 15.09, 
15.10, 15.12, 15.16, 15.22, 15.24 and 15.26 of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the 
Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, updating specified Building Code 
requirements and making technical corrections as further specified. Reporting for Public Services 
Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal. Motion 
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. Public comment was made by: 
Michael White. 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending various sections within Chapter 8.12, 
International Fire Code adopted, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming, updating the edition of the International Fire Code adopted by the City 
and further amending as specified. Mr. White moved to approve on second reading, seconded by 
Dr. Emmons. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich.  
 
 

2 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending section 8.24.020 of the Municipal Code of 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, clarifying and further regulating the installation of above-ground 
tanks and containers as specified. Mr. White moved to approve on second reading, seconded by 
Mr. Esquibel. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Repealing the provisions for the Paid On-Street Parking 
Districts program of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, further amending 
the regulatory authority governing parking in Cheyenne and abrogating any On-Street Parking 
District designated by Resolution of the Governing Body – referred to Public Services Committee.  
(Sponsors – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Moody and Mr. White)   
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Sections 1.4.4, 5.1.4 and 5.7.10 of the Unified 
Development Code to remove the land use term ‘Work Camps’ and prohibit their establishment 
within the City – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsors – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Moody, Dr. 
Rinne, Mr. Segrave and Mr. White) 
 
 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Approving the ‘2024 Cheyenne DDA Plan of Development’ presented 
by the Downtown Development Authority of the City of Cheyenne – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsors – Mr. Roybal and Mr. Segrave) 
 
[CA] 
Lease Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Girl Scouts of Montana and 
Wyoming, for 45.93 acres of city-owned real property located near the former Roundtop Water 
Treatment Facility to be used for the purposes of conducting activities consistent with the By-Laws 
and Articles of Incorporation of the Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming (Revenue to the City). 
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount of $100.00). (#8131) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and TDSi - The Design 
Studio, inc., for professional architecture and engineering services for various projects at the 
Historic Cheyenne Depot and Depot Plaza (2021 Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund) – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #8070 with Wyoming Department of Health, Aging 
Division to provide transportation support services to persons 60 years or older under the Title 
IIIB Program Grant for FY25 (IIIB Division on Aging Grant). Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried (in an amount of $10,192.00). (#8070) 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #3 to Contract #7531 between the City of Cheyenne and Waste 
Management for Recycling Services through April 15, 2026 (Solid Waste Fund) – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #2 to Contract #7447 between the City of Cheyenne and HDR 
Engineering, Inc., for the 5th Street Bridge over Crow Creek Design Project (2019-2022 Optional 
1% Sales Tax Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Amendment #6 to Contract #2985 between the City of Cheyenne and the City of Cheyenne-
Laramie County Joint Powers Board for use of premises within Frontier Park – referred to Finance 
Committee. 

3 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #4 to Contract #6441 between the City of Cheyenne and Microsoft 
Corporation for the renewal to the City’s Enterprise Office 365 Online Enrollment (General Fund) 
– referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid # S-11-25 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
Academy Sports Turf, Inc., for the Pioneer Park Baseball Field Synthetic Turf Replacement 
Project (One Time State Distribution Funds, Community Facility Fees). Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $212,700.00). (#8132) 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-6-25 for 95 and 65 Gallon Residential Autocarts (trash cans) for 
the Sanitation Division (Solid Waste Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration to purchase between the City of Cheyenne and Axon Enterprise, Inc. for the 
purchase of a 60-month subscription for ten (10) Axon Fleet-3 in-car cameras, peripheral 
hardware, software, licensing and warranties for the City of Cheyenne Police Department (General 
Fund Reserves) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 
Governing body comment was made by Mr. Moody on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, February 24, 2025 at 
6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming, and via electronic 
conference meeting, for renewal applications for existing City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, 
limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, microbrewery, satellite distillery, special malt beverage 
and winery permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office. 
 
 
Announcement was made that due to the President’s Day Holiday on Monday, February 
17, 2025, the regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held in the 
Municipal Building, Room 104, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic 
conference meeting as follows: Public Services Committee will meet Tuesday, February 18, 2025 
at Noon and Finance Committee will meet Wednesday, February 19, 2025 at Noon. 
 
 
Public comments were made by the following under Other Business: Corey Lynn Loghry, 
Scott Hahn, Astrid, Michelle Shimmin, Virginia Dandy Brinkerhoff and Veronica (no last name 
provided). 
 
 
The following members of the governing body made comments under Other Business: Dr. 
Aldrich and Mr. Moody. 
 
 
Dr. Rinne moved to meet in executive session pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16-4- 
405(a)(iii)(x), seconded by Mr. Roybal. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. Mayor Collins announced members would meet in executive session, not 
open to the public and stated there would be no action taken by the governing body as a result of 
the executive session, and that the regular City Council meeting would officially conclude at the 
close of the executive session. 
 
 

4 
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:24 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kylie Soden, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
February 19, 2025  
 
 
 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-02-04_TotalExtraction_14852303.pdf
CITY CLERK’S OFFICE
2101 O’Neil Ave., Room 101
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001
(307) 637-6346
M E M O R A N D U M
TO:
Mayor Patrick Collins
Members of City Council
FROM:
Kylie Soden, City Clerk
DATE:
February 20, 2025
RE:
City Liquor License Renewals for 2025-2026
Attached is a listing of those applicants applying for renewal of existing City liquor licenses
(i.e.: retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar & grill licenses, microbrewery, satellite distillery, 
special malt beverage and winery permits), liquor licensee actions that occurred during this past
licensing year and the Police Department’s compliance report.  Public hearing for the applications
is scheduled during the February 24, 2025 City Council meeting. Please note, the renewal for F &
G Corporation d/b/a The Green Door will have a separate approval schedule as the licensee
is also requesting to transfer the location of the parked license and the transfer must be done
before the renewal.
Due to the large volume of paperwork involved in renewing the liquor licenses, the attached
listing provides the “basics” from application information in a readable format.  If you would like
to review an individual application file, please contact me and I will make it available to you.
If you have any questions concerning applicant information or renewal requirements and
procedures, please do not hesitate to contact me at your convenience.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-02-04_TotalExtraction_48252876.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins, President Rinne, and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Eric Fountain, Director of Compliance Division 
Antony (Tono) Pomerleau, Chief Building Official 
Subject: 2024 Code Adoption  
"AN ORDINANCE AMENDING VARIOUS SECTIONS OF CHAPTER 15.08, 
15.09, 15.10, 15.12, 15.16, 15.22, 15.24 AND 15.26 OF TITLE 15, BUILDINGS 
AND CONSTRUCTION, OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, UPDATING SPECIFIED BUILDING CODE 
REQUIREMENTS AND MAKING TECHNICAL CORRECTIONS AS FURTHER 
SPECIFIED." 
Date: 
February 3, 2025 
 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the governing body approve the 2024 Code Adoption "AN ORDINANCE 
AMENDING VARIOUS SECTIONS OF CHAPTER 15.08, 15.09, 15.10, 15.12, 15.16, 
15.22, 15.24 AND 15.26 OF TITLE 15, BUILDINGS AND CONSTRUCTION, OF THE 
MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, UPDATING 
SPECIFIED BUILDING CODE REQUIREMENTS AND MAKING TECHNICAL 
CORRECTIONS AS FURTHER SPECIFIED." 
 
 
 
Background:  
 
Mr. Mayor, Mr. President, Members of the Council, 
The item for consideration proposed with this ordinance is the adoption of the 2024 International Codes 
and ordinances with modifications for the City of Cheyenne.   
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
City Council – January 27th  
• 
Public Services Committee – February 3rd  
• 
City Council – February 10th  
• 
Public Services Committee – February 17th  
• 
City Council – February 24th  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Building Safety Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6265 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

 
 
 
State Requirements to maintain jurisdiction: 
 
Jurisdictions are required to adopt minimum standards by ordinance or resolution that are equivalent to 
or more stringent than those standards adopted by the Wyoming Department of Fire and Electrical 
Safety (State).  The State concluded adoption of the new International Codes and began enforcement on 
June 28th, 2024.  Jurisdictions are required to adopt these updated standards within 6 months of the 
adoption date by the State.  The current standards adopted by the State include: 
 
2024 International Fire Code (IFC) 
2024 International Building Code (IBC) 
2024 International Fuel Gas Code (IFGC) 
2024 International Mechanical Code (IMC) 
2024 International Existing Building Code (IEBC) 
2023 National Electrical Code (NEC) 
 
Process: 
The Compliance Department Building Safety Division has requested involvement from the community to 
work through the code adoption processes and the Contractor/City 2024 Code Adoption Committee was 
formed and is consisted of the following members; 
 
• 
Contractors 
• 
Dan Edeen  
(Phoenix Builders) 
 
• 
Eric Adams 
(Gateway Construction) 
• 
Jeff Story 
(ACSI) 
• 
Royce Fertig 
(5R Construction 
• 
Justin Montgomery 
(Design Point Engineering) 
• 
Julie Goode 
(Benchmark Engineering) 
• 
Jason Miller 
(Moore Insulation) 
• 
Will Byers 
(TDSI) 
• 
City Staff 
• 
Eric Fountain (Compliance Director) 
• 
Marilyn Sutherland (Deputy Compliance Director) 
• 
John Brodie (City Attorney) 
• 
Tono Pomerleau (Chief Building Official) 
• 
Bob Mason (Fire Marshal) 
• 
Devin Collins (Deputy Building Official) 
• 
Sean Delaney (Inspector) 
• 
Robert Alcon (Inspector) 
 
Meetings; 
 
Contractor/City 2024 Code Adoption Committee met on the following dates; 
-December 3rd, 2024 
-December 10th, 2024 
-December 17, 2024 
-January 7th, 2025 

 
 
During these meetings the committee collaborated and engaged in many discussions regarding all of the 
following; 
 
-review of all significant changes relevant to all new codes being considered for code adoption. 
-Energy code (IECC) identified as cost prohibitive for adoption by the city of Cheyenne due to 
many changes over the last 2 code cycles adding significant costs to construction with no net 
gain to the residential and construction industry within the City of Cheyenne jurisdiction. 
Further review and discussion surrounding the continued adoption of the 2018 energy code 
(IECC). 
-finalizing discussions concerning the energy code adoption to remain the 2018 code.  
 
 
Proposed Code/Ordinance revisions: 
 
General items   
 
• 
For all sections in ordinance multiple administrative and housekeeping changes are required for 
code and ordinance updates.  This also includes section reference updates.   
 
Section 1 IBC (International Building Code) 
 
• 
Adoption of the 2024 code with no changes to the ordinance or code noted.  
 
Section 2 IEBC (International Existing Building Code) 
 
• 
Adoption of the 2024 code with no changes to the ordinance or code noted.  
 
Section 3 IRC (International Residential Code) 
 
• 
Adoption of the 2024 code with no changes to the ordinance or code noted.  
 
Section 4 IPC (International Plumbing Code) 
 
• 
Adoption of the 2024 code with no changes to the ordinance or code noted.  
 
Section 5 IMC (International Mechanical Code) 
 
• 
Adoption of the 2024 code with no changes to the ordinance or code noted.  
 
Section 6 IFGC (International Fuel Gas Code) 
 
• 
Adoption of the 2024 code with no changes to the ordinance or code noted.  
 
Section 7 IPMC (International Property Maintenance Code) 
 
• 
Adoption of the 2024 code with no changes to the ordinance or code noted.  
 
 

Section 8 IECC (International Energy Conservation Code) 
 
• 
Adoption of the 2018 code with the following changes to the ordinance and code noted.  
• 
Section I has been modified to allow for alternative standards to be utilized when testing smaller 
(affordable) housing where the total square footage (s.f.) is 1500 s.f. or less. 
• 
Section T has been modified to allow for commercial structures with vestibules that do not have 
a building automation system to be allowed to utilize a stand-alone thermostat for controls. 
   
 
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that the code adoption of the 2024 International Codes have been reviewed with all 
significant changes and ordinance changes having been presented to the public at large for full review and 
public comment.   
 
 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING VARIOUS SECTIONS OF 
CHAPTER 15.08, 15.09, 15.10, 15.12, 15.16, 15.22, 15.24 AND 15.26 OF TITLE 15, 
BUILDINGS AND CONSTRUCTION, OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, UPDATING SPECIFIED BUILDING CODE 
REQUIREMENTS AND MAKING TECHNICAL CORRECTIONS AS FURTHER 
SPECIFIED." 
 
 

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING VARIOUS SECTIONS OF CHAPTER 
15.08, 15.09, 15.10, 15.12, 15.16, 15.22, 15.24 AND 15.26 OF TITLE 15, 
BUILDINGS AND CONSTRUCTION, OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, UPDATING SPECIFIED 
BUILDING CODE REQUIREMENTS AND MAKING TECHNICAL 
CORRECTIONS AS FURTHER SPECIFIED." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Sections 15.08.010, International Building Code—Adopted, and 
15.08.020, Amendments, of Chapter 15, International Building Code Adopted, of Title 15, 
Buildings and Construction, of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, are hereby amended as set out in 
this section: 
 
15.08.010 - International Building Code—Adopted. 
 
 
A document, being marked and designated as provided in this section and of which once one 
copy is on file in the office of the city clerk and open for inspection of the public, is adopted and 
made a part hereof as if fully set out in this chapter: 
 
International Building Code, 2021 2024 Edition, as published by the International Code 
Council, Inc., including Appendices A, G, H, I, J, and K; 
 
15.08.020 - Amendments. 
 
 
The International Building Code adopted in Section 15.08.010 above is amended as follows: 
 
A. Section 101.1. Insert: The City of Cheyenne; for [NAME OF JURISDICTION]. 
 
B. Section 105.5 is deleted and re-created to read as follows: 
 
Every permit issued shall expire and become invalid one year after the date of issuance. 
The chief building official is authorized to grant one 180-day renewal permit, for a non-
expired permit, subject to written request by the permit holder. If approved, a renewal 
permit is subject to an additional permit fee based upon fifteen percent (15%) of the 
original permit valuation. 
 
Exception: Large projects, as approved by the chief building official, which cannot 
logically be completed within twelve (12) months and/or where a contractual 
completion date is in excess of twelve (12) months. 
 
C. Section 105.2, work exempt from permit, subsections 1. and 2. under the heading 
"building" are amended to read as follows: 
3:57 pm, Jan 21, 2025

Page 2 of 24 
 
 
1. One-story detached accessory structures used as tool and storage sheds, playhouses, 
and similar uses, provided the floor area is not greater than 200 square feet. 
 
2. Fences not over 6 feet high. 
 
Remaining sections under the heading "building" contained within Section 105.2 
remain unchanged. 
 
D. Section 107.1 is amended by the addition of a second paragraph to read as follows: 
 
Drainage plans shall be submitted for all building permit applications for all new 
commercial construction. Drainage plans shall be prepared by a Wyoming Licensed 
Professional Engineer (P.E.) or Professional Land Surveyor (P.L.S.). Drainage plans 
shall indicate, with drainage arrows, the drainage patterns for the site. Drainage away 
from the structure shall be in conformance with this code. Site grading and drainage 
shall conform to the approved grading or drainage plan for the subdivision where the 
site is located. All drainage easements on the lot shall be shown on the drainage plan. 
All FEMA-regulated floodplain or city-delineated special flood hazard area boundaries 
shall also be shown on the drainage plan. No variation from the approved grading or 
drainage plan for the subdivision where the site is located will be granted an exception, 
except by written authorization from the city engineer or his designated representative. 
 
E. Section 109.2 is amended by the addition of a final sentence to read as follows: 
 
Fees shall be as indicated in Section 15.08.030 of the Cheyenne City Code. 
 
F. Section 109.2.1 is created to read as follows: 
 
Reinspection's. A reinspection fee may be assessed for each inspection or reinspection 
when such portion of the work for which inspection is called is not complete or when 
corrections called for are not made. 
 
This section is not to be interpreted as requiring reinspection fees the first time a job is 
rejected for failure to comply with the requirements of this code, but as controlling the 
practice of calling for inspections before the job is ready for such inspection or 
reinspection. 
 
Reinspection fees may be assessed when the inspection record card is not posted or 
otherwise available on the work site, the approved plans are not readily available to the 
deviating from plans requiring the approval of the building official. 
 
To obtain a reinspection, the applicant shall file an application therefore in writing on 
a form furnished for that purpose and pay the reinspection fee in accordance with 
Section 15.08.030. 
 

Page 3 of 24 
 
In instances where reinspection fees have been assessed, no additional inspection of the 
work will be performed until the required fees have been paid. 
 
G. Section 109.4 is amended by the addition of a final sentence to read as follows: 
 
Said fee is an investigation fee and shall be in an amount equal to the permit fee. 
 
H. Section 109.6 is deleted and re-created to read as follows: 
 
Fee refunds shall be as specified in Section 15.08.040 of the Cheyenne City Code. 
 
I. Section 111.2 is amended to read as follows: 
 
1. Delete the first paragraph and re-create it to read as follows: 
 
Certificate Issued. After the chief building official inspects the building or structure 
and finds no violations of the provisions of this code or other laws that are enforced 
by the department of building safety and has received proof of compliance with the 
development office site plan of record and has received certification from a 
Wyoming Licensed Professional Engineer (P.E.) or Professional Land Surveyor 
(P.L.S.) that the approved drainage plan conforms with this code and the approved 
grading or drainage plan of the subdivision where the site is located, with his or her 
original seal and signature affixed, the chief building official shall issue a certificate 
of occupancy that contains the following: (Remainder of Section 112.2 111.2 
unchanged). 
 
J. Section 113 is deleted in its entirety and re-created to read as follows: 
 
Appeals shall be made to the board of appeals as prescribed by Section 15.04.020 of 
the Cheyenne City Code. 
 
K. Section 114.4 is amended by the addition of a final sentence to read as follows: 
 
Penalties shall be as specified in Section 1.24.010 of the Cheyenne City Code. 
 
L. Chapter 13, Energy Efficiency, is amended to read as follows: 
 
1301.1.1 Criteria. Buildings shall be designed and constructed in accordance with the 
International Energy Conservation Code, 2018 Edition, as adopted in City Code 
Chapter 15.26. 
 
M. Section 1512.2.1.1 is amended by deleting Exception Number 3 and recreating 
Exception Number 3 to read as follows: 
 
3. Where the existing roof has one or more applications of asphalt shingles as the City 
of Cheyenne is considered to be in an area subject to moderate hail exposure. 

Page 4 of 24 
 
 
N. Section 1608.2, Ground snow loads, is amended by the addition of a final sentence to 
read as follows: 
 
The ground snow load for the City of Cheyenne shall be thirty (30) pounds per square 
foot. 
 
O. Section 1609.4, Exposure category, is amended by adding a first sentence to read as 
follows: 
 
The exposure category for the City of Cheyenne is Exposure C. 
 
P. Section 1612.3. Insert: The Laramie County, Wyoming and Incorporated Areas of the 
City of Cheyenne; for [NAME OF JURISDICTION] and January 17, 2007 for [DATE 
OF ISSUANCE]. 
 
Q. Appendix J, Grading, is amended as follows: 
 
Section J103.1 is deleted and re-created to read as follows: 
 
Permits required. Except as exempted in Section J103.2, no grading shall be performed 
without first having obtained a permit from the city engineer's office. A grading permit 
does not include the construction of retaining walls or other structures. 
 
The following sections are hereby created to read as follows: 
 
Section J103.1.1 Grading Fees. Fees shall be assessed in accordance with the provisions 
of this section. 
 
Section J103.1.2 Plan Review Fees. When a plan or other data are required to be 
submitted, a plan review fee shall be paid at the time of submitting plans and 
specifications for review. Said plan review fee shall be as set forth in Table J103.1. 
Separate plan review fees shall apply to retaining walls or major drainage structures as 
required elsewhere in this code. For excavation and fill on the same site, the fee shall 
be based on the volume of excavation or fill, whichever is greater. 
 
Section J103.1.3 Grading Permit Fees. A fee for each grading permit shall be paid as 
set forth in Table J103.1. Separate permits and fees shall apply to retaining walls or 
major drainage structures as required elsewhere in this code. There shall be no separate 
charge for standard terrace drains and similar facilities.

Drainage plans shall be submitted for all building permit applications for all new

Section 4.  That Sections 15.12.010, International Plumbing Code—Adopted, and 
15.12.020, Amendments, of Chapter 15.12, International Plumbing Code Adopted, of Title 15, 
Buildings and Construction, of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, are hereby amended as set out in 
this section: 
 
15.12.010 - International Plumbing Code—Adopted. 
 
A document, being marked and designated as provided in this section and of which one copy 
is on file in the office of the city clerk and open for inspection of the public, is adopted and made 
a part hereof as if fully set out in this chapter: 
 
International Plumbing Code, 2021 2024 Edition, as published by the International Code 
Council, Inc., including Appendices B, C, D, AND E; 
 
15.12.020 - Amendments. 
 
The International Plumbing Code adopted in Section 15.12.010 above is amended as follows: 
 
A. Section 101.1. Insert: The City of Cheyenne; for [NAME OF JURISDICTION]. 
 
B. Section 109.2. 108.2. Fees shall be as indicated in Section 15.08.030 of the Cheyenne 
City Code. 
 
C. Section 109.5 108.6 is deleted and re-created to read as follows: 
 
Fee refunds shall be as specified in Section 15.08.040 of the Cheyenne City Code. 
 
D. Section 108.4.3.1 111.4.3.1 is created to read as follows: 
 
Reinspections. A reinspection fee may be assessed for each inspection or reinspection 
when such portion of the work for which inspection is called is not complete or when 
corrections called for are not made. 
 
This section is not to be interpreted as requiring reinspection fees the first time a job is 
rejected for failure to comply with the requirements of this code, but as controlling the 
practice of calling for inspections before the job is ready for such inspection or 
reinspection. 
 
Reinspection fees may be assessed when the inspection record card is not posted or 
otherwise available on the work site, the approved plans are not readily available to the 
inspector, for failure to provide access on the date for which inspection is requested, or 
for deviating from plans requiring the approval of the building official. 
 

Page 13 of 24 
 
To obtain a reinspection, the applicant shall file an application therefore in writing on 
a form furnished for that purpose and pay the reinspection fee in accordance with 
Section 15.08.030. 
 
In instances where reinspection fees have been assessed, no additional inspection of the 
work will be performed until the required fees have been paid. 
 
E. Section 109.3 108.4 is deleted and re-created to read as follows: 
 
Any person who shall violate a provision of this code or who shall fail to comply with 
any of the requirements thereof or who shall erect, install, alter or repair plumbing work 
in violation of the approved construction documents, directive of the code official or of 
a permit or certificate issued under the provisions of this code shall be subject to 
penalties as specified in Section 1.24.010 of the Cheyenne City Code. 
 
F. Section 113.4. 115.4 The last sentence is amended to read as follows: 
 
Any person who shall continue any work after having been served with a stop work 
order, except such work as that person is directed to perform to remove a violation or 
unsafe condition, shall be subject to penalties as prescribed by law. 
 
G. Section 109 112 is deleted in its entirety and re-created to read as follows: 
 
Appeals shall be made to the board of appeals as prescribed by Section 15.04.020 of 
the Cheyenne City Code. 
 
H. Section 115.4 114.4 is deleted and recreated to read as follows: 
 
[A] 115.4 114.4 Violation penalties. Any person who shall violate a provision of this 
code or shall fail to comply with any of the requirements thereof or who shall erect, 
install, alter or repair plumbing work in violation of the approved construction 
documents or directive of the code official, or of a permit or certificate issued under 
the provisions of this code, shall be subject to penalties as specified in Section 1.24.010 
of the Cheyenne City Code.

I. Section R402.4.1.2 is amended to read as follows: 
 
R402.4.1.2 Testing. The building or dwelling unit shall be tested and verified as having 
an air leakage rate of not exceeding 5 air changes per hour in Climate Zones 1 and 2, 
and 4 air changes per hour in Climate Zones 3 through 8. Testing shall be conducted 
with a blower door at a pressure of 0.2 inches W.G. (50 pascals). in accordance with 
RESNET/ICC 380, ASTM E779 or ASTM E1827 and reported at a pressure of 0.2 inch 
w.g. (50 Pascals). Where required by the code official, testing shall be conducted by an 
approved third party. A written report of the results of the test shall be signed by the 
party conducting the test and provided to the code official. Testing shall be performed 
at any time after creation of all penetrations of the building thermal envelope. 
 
Exception: When testing individual dwelling units, an air leakage rate not exceeding 
0.30 cubic feet per minute per square foot [0.008 m3/(s × m2)] of the dwelling unit 
enclosure area, tested in accordance with ANSI/RESNET/ICC 380, ASTM E779 or 
ASTM E1827 and reported at a pressure of 0.2 inch w.g. (50 Pa), shall be an accepted 
alternative permitted in all climate zones for: 
 
1. Attached single and multiple-family building dwelling units; and 
 
2. Buildings or dwelling units that are 1,500 square feet (139.4 m2) or smaller. 
 
The remainder of Section 402.4.1.2 is unchanged.

a. R-values are minimums. U-factors and SHGC are maximums. R-19 batts compressed 
into a nominal 2 × 6 framing cavity such that the R-value is reduced by R-1 or more shall be 
marked with the compressed batt R-value in addition to the full thickness R-value. 
 
b. The fenestration U-factor column excludes skylights. The SHGC column applies to all 
glazed fenestration. 
 
c. "15/19" means R-15 continuous insulated sheathing on the interior or exterior of the home 
or R-19 cavity insulation at the interior of the basement wall. "15/19" shall be permitted to be 
met with R-13 cavity insulation on the interior of the basement wall plus R-5 continuous 
insulated sheathing on the interior or exterior of the home. "10/13" means R-10 continuous 
insulated sheathing on the interior or exterior of the home or R-13 cavity insulation at the 
interior of the basement wall. 
 
d. R-5 shall be added to the required slab edge R-values for heated slabs. Insulation depth 
shall be the depth of the footing or 2 feet, whichever is less in Zones 1 through 3 for heated 
slabs. 
 
e. Or insulation sufficient to fill the framing cavity, R-19 minimum. 
 
f. "13+5" means R-13 cavity insulation plus R-5 insulated sheathing. If structural sheathing 
covers 25 percent or less of the exterior, insulating sheathing is not required where structural 
sheathing is used. If structural sheathing covers more than 25 percent of exterior, structural 
sheathing shall be supplemented with insulated sheathing of at least R-2. 
 
g. The second R-value applies when more than half the insulation is on the interior of the 
mass wall.

L. Table R402.4.1.1 is amended to read as follows: 
 
Table R402.4.1.1 
Air Barrier and Insulation Inspection Component Criteria 
 
Component  
Criteria  
Air barrier and thermal barrier  
Exterior thermal envelope insulation for framed walls is 
installed in substantial contact and continuous 
alignment with building envelope air barrier.  
Breaks or joints in the air barrier and filled or repaired.  
Air-permeable insulation is not used as a sealing 
material.  
Air-permeable insulation is inside of an air barrier.  
Ceiling/attic  
Air barrier in any dropped ceiling/soffit is substantially 
aligned with insulation and any gaps are sealed.  
Attic access (except unvented attic), knee wall door, or 
drop-down stair is sealed.  
Walls  
Corners and headers are insulated.  
Junction of foundation and sill plate is sealed.  
Windows and doors  
Space between window/door jambs and framing is 
sealed.  
Rim joists  
Rim joists are insulated and include an air barrier.  
Floors  
(including above-garage and 
cantilevered floors)  
Insulation is installed to maintain permanent contact 
with underside of subfloor decking.  
Air barrier is installed at any exposed edge of 
insulation.  
Crawl space walls  
Insulation is permanently attached to walls.  
Exposed earth in unvented crawl spaces is covered with 
Class I vapor retarder with overlapping joints taped.  
Shafts, penetrations  
Duct shafts, utility penetrations, knee walls and flue 
shafts opening to exterior or unconditioned space are 
sealed.  
Narrow cavities  
Batts in narrow cavities are cut to fit, or narrow cavities 
are filled by sprayed/blown insulation.  

Page 23 of 24 
 
Garage separation  
Air sealing is provided between the garage and 
conditioned spaces.  
Recessed lighting  
Recessed light fixtures are airtight, IC rated, and sealed 
to drywall.  
Exception — fixtures in conditioned space.  
Plumbing and wiring  
Insulation is placed between outside and pipes. Batt 
insulation is cut to fit around wiring and plumbing, or 
sprayed/blown insulation extends behind piping and 
wiring.  
Shower/tub on exterior wall  
Showers and tubs on exterior walls have insulation and

8.12.010 - Purpose—Authority—General provisions. 
 
The purpose of this chapter is to adopt the International Fire Code, 2021 2024 Edition, as an 
enforceable regulation governing the safeguarding of life and property from fire and explosion 
hazards arising from the storage, handling and use of hazardous substances, materials, and devices, 
and from conditions hazardous to life or property in the occupancy of buildings and premises. 
 
8.12.020 - International Fire Code—Adopted.

is on file in the office of the city clerk and open for inspection of the public, is adopted and made

International Fire Code, 2021 2024 Edition, as published by the International Code 
Council, Inc., including Appendices A through N.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-02-04_TotalExtraction_10154116.pdf
ORDINANCE NO.     
 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 8.24.020 OF THE MUNICIPAL 
CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, CLARIFYING AND 
FURTHER REGULATING THE INSTALLATION OF ABOVE-GROUND 
TANKS AND CONTAINERS AS SPECIFIED." 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That Section 8.24.020, Aboveground Tanks in City Limits, of Chapter 8.24, 
Flammable and Combustible Liquids, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, is hereby amended to read: 
 
8.24.020 
Above-ground tanks and containers in city limits. 
 
A. It is unlawful to have aboveground storage tanks exceeding sixty (60) gallons except in a 
heavy industrial (HI), light industrial (LI) or public (P) zone with the approval of, and under 
conditions established by, the chief of the Cheyenne fire department. Such tanks exceeding 
sixty (60) gallons The following above-ground tanks and containers may only be installed 
in the city with the written approval of, and subject to the conditions established by, the 
fire code official: 
 
1. Any above-ground tank with a capacity of more than sixty (60) gallons that stores or is 
intended to store any Class I or Class II liquid; and 
 
2. Any above-ground containers with a capacity of sixty (60) gallons or less that stores or 
is intended to store any Class I or Class II liquid. 
 
B. An above-ground tank located in any refinery complexes and complex or bulk plants plant 
shall be in compliance with the National Fire Protection Association Pamphlet No. NFPA 
30, and the International Fire Code as adopted under chapter 8.24, title 8, of the city code. 
 
 
Section 2.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 3.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-02-04_TotalExtraction_75177451.pdf
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE REPEALING THE PROVISIONS FOR THE PAID 
ON-STREET PARKING DISTRICTS PROGRAM OF THE MUNICIPAL 
CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, FURTHER 
AMENDING 
THE 
REGULATORY 
AUTHORITY 
GOVERNING 
PARKING IN CHEYENNE AND ABROGATING ANY ON-STREET 
PARKING DISTRICT DESIGNATED BY RESOLUTION OF THE 
GOVERNING BODY." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 10.52.220, Parking Time Limited, of Chapter 10.52, Stopping, 
Standing and Parking Generally, and Section 10.60.010, Signed Time Limitations, of Chapter 
10.60, Parking Limitations, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
is hereby amended as set out in this section: 
 
10.52.220 - Parking time limited. 
 
 
When parking time limitation signs are erected in each block giving notice thereof, no person 
shall park a vehicle for longer than the time specified on posted signs on any day except Sundays 
and public holidays, unless otherwise indicated upon any of the streets within the city as designated 
by the city traffic engineer. Any person who has parked a vehicle in an area where parking is 
limited by time shall before the expiration of that time move the vehicle as required by Section 
10.60.010 of the city code. The requirements imposed under this section shall not apply to paid 
on-street parking districts as provided by Section 10.60.015 of the city code. from the spot in which 
the vehicle was previously parked before the expiration of the time indicated on the posted signs. 
 
10.60.010 - Signed time limitations. 
 
Any person who has parked a vehicle in an area where parking is limited by time shall move 
from the block face of the vehicle's parked position the vehicle from the spot in which the vehicle 
was previously parked before the expiration of the time indicated on the posted signs. This 
requirement applies on any day except Sundays and public holidays or unless otherwise indicated 
upon any of the streets within the city as designated by the city traffic engineer. The requirements 
imposed under this section shall not apply to paid on-street parking districts as provided by Section 
10.60.015 of this chapter. As used in this section, "block face" means the one side of a specific 
city block where signed time limited parking is available. 
 
 
Section 2.  That Section 10.60.015, Paid On-Street Parking Districts; Establishment; 
Administration, of Chapter 10.60, Parking Limitations, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby repealed. 
 
10.60.015 - Paid on-street parking districts; establishment; administration. 
 
9:49 am, Feb 04, 2025

Page 2 of 4 
 
A. There is created the paid on-street parking districts program, which shall be administered 
by the parking division within the Cheyenne police department in accordance with this 
section. The geographic area of any paid on-street parking district administered under this 
program shall be designated by resolution of the governing body. 
 
B. Within each paid on-street parking district as designated by the governing body, the 
parking administration manager for the division shall establish appropriate on-street 
parking zones and reasonable on-street parking rates. The purpose for establishing any 
parking zone or parking rate shall be to positively influence parking behavior, prioritizing 
customer and visitor parking while emphasizing off-street options for longer-term parkers, 
such business employees and city residents. When establishing any parking zone or any 
parking rate under this subsection, the parking administration manager shall take the 
following factors into consideration: 
 
1. The types of activities or businesses to be encompassed within the proposed zone and 
whether the proposed parking rate will support the efficient use of the available on-
street parking; 
 
2. Whether dynamic parking management is appropriate for a proposed zone in order to 
manage demand for on-street parking through variable parking rates and dissemination 
of real-time parking availability information; and 
 
3. Other parking conditions, such as availability and usage trends, for the purpose of 
tailoring the paid on-street parking districts program to better match the specific needs 
and dynamics of a parking zone and between zones to optimize parking management 
and resource utilization. 
 
C. Before any parking zone or parking rate is established under subsection (B) of this section, 
and before any parking zone or parking rate may be modified by the parking administration 
manager, the division shall provide public notice of the proposed parking zone or parking 
rate, or modification thereof, by means of publication of the notice on not less than one (1) 
City of Cheyenne media platform. In addition, the notice required under this subsection 
shall be provided for a period of not less than thirty (30) days before the proposed parking 
zone or parking rate establishment or modification is set to take effect and shall seek to 
solicit public input on the proposal. 
 
D. Every parking zone established under this section shall include appropriate signage 
communicating the type of zone and the authorized payment methods as provided by the 
division. Authorized payment methods may include payments through electronic means 
including using webpages, smartphone applications or payment kiosks. The division shall 
require all persons wanting to park a vehicle within a paid on-street parking district to first 
register for a user account for the purposes of program administration and securing on-
street parking payments. 
 
E. Information regarding every current parking district and parking zone, along with all 
attendant parking rates, shall be made publicly available by the division. The information 

Page 3 of 4 
 
required under this subsection shall include maps for any parking district and parking zone 
established under this section and shall be publicly accessible on a City of Cheyenne media 
platform. 
 
F. It shall constitute a violation of this section, punishable by a civil fine as provided by 
Section 10.60.040 of this chapter, for any person to park a vehicle in a designated parking 
location within a paid on-street parking district: 
 
1. Without first registering for a user account as required under subsection (D) of this 
section; or 
 
2. Having registered for a user account, and parked in excess of the time authorized for 
the person to park the vehicle as provided by the paid parking apparatus or time 
monitoring platform regulating vehicle parking is the particular parking zone. 
 
G. As used in this section: 
 
1. "Division" means the parking division within the Cheyenne police department; 
 
2. "On-street parking rate" or "parking rate" means the monetary charge levied by the City 
of Cheyenne for the privilege of parking a vehicle for a specified duration in a 
designated parking space within any paid on-street parking district; 
 
3. "On-street parking zone" or "parking zone" means the geographical area located within 
a parking district that is specified in accordance with this section, which exhibits 
consistent usage characteristics that allow for the assessment of parking patterns and 
determination of parking rates based on parking demand; and 
 
4. "Paid on-street parking district" or "parking district" means the geographical area 
specified by resolution of the governing body for the implementation of paid on-street 
parking as provided by this section. 
 
Section 3.  Any paid on-street parking district designated by resolution of the governing 
body in accordance with the authority granted under Section 10.60.015 of the city code, including 
Resolution No.6441, adopted by the governing body on November 25, 2024, which authority is 
hereby repealed by section 2 of this ordinance, shall be deemed void and otherwise considered 
repealed upon the effective provided herein. 
 
Section 4.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 5.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication. 
 
 

Page 4 of 4
